<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For a prime <i>p</i> chosen uniformly from $`3\le p\le x`$, list the prime factors of $`p-1`$ in decreasing order, with multiplicity. As $`x\to\infty`$, their logarithms, divided by $`\log(p-1)`$, converge in every finite joint distribution to the Poisson–Dirichlet distribution with parameter one. This proves the conjecture of Ford, Konyagin and Luca.

- **claim-main-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed $k\ge1$ and every bounded continuous function $F:[0,1]^k\to\mathbb R$, $$\label{eq:main-law} \lim_{x\to\infty}\frac1{\pi(x)-1} \sum_{\substack{3\le p\le x\\p\text{ prime}}} F\bigl(V_1(p),\ldots,V_k(p)\bigr) =\mathbb EF(L_1,\ldots,L_k).$$ The limit holds through all real $x$ and uses ordinary equal weighting of the primes.
  - Explanation: The ranked logarithmic sizes of the prime factors of a uniformly chosen shifted prime converge in every finite joint distribution to the Poisson–Dirichlet law of parameter one.
- **claim-prime-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prime-estimates)): The prime number theorem holds with an error smaller than every fixed negative power of the logarithm. In particular, as $y\to\infty$, $$\sum_{p\le y}\frac1p=\log\log y+c_M+o(1), \qquad \prod_{p\le y}\left(1-\frac1p\right) \sim\frac{e^{-\gamma_E}}{\log y},$$ where $c_M$ and $\gamma_E$ are constants. For fixed $A,C>0$, uniformly for $r\le(\log y)^C$ and $(a,r)=1$, $$\#\{p\le y:p\equiv a\pmod r\} =\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Li}}(y)}{\varphi(r)} +O_{A,C}\!\left(\frac{y}{(\log y)^A}\right).$$ The constants need not be effective.
  - Explanation: Collects the classical prime counting, Mertens-type and progression estimates with strong error terms; the constants are not effective.
- **claim-moment-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-moment-bounds)): For every fixed nonnegative integer $k$ there is $C_k$ such that $$\sum_{n\le Y}\tau(n)^k\ll_k Y(\log(2Y))^{C_k},\qquad \sum_{n\le Y}\frac{\tau(n)^k}{n} \ll_k(\log(2Y))^{C_k}.$$ Let $P(t)=\sum_{n\le Y}c_n n^{it}$. On every real interval $I$ of length $T\ge0$, $$\begin{aligned} \int_I\|P(t)\|^2\,\mathrm dt &\ll (T+Y\log(2Y))\sum_n\|c_n\|^2,\label{eq:dirichlet-mean}\\ \sum_{t\in\mathcal T}\|P(t)\|^2 &\ll (T+1+Y\log(2Y))(\log(2Y))^2 \sum_n\|c_n\|^2,\label{eq:dirichlet-spaced} \end{aligned}$$ whenever $\mathcal T\subset I$ has mutual spacing at least one. The constants in these two inequalities are absolute and do not depend on how the coefficients were formed.
  - Explanation: Gives divisor power sum bounds and coefficient-independent mean square bounds for Dirichlet polynomials on intervals and on well-spaced point sets.
- **claim-long-prime-polynomial** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-long-prime)): Fix $0<\tau<\eta<1$ and $C,A>0$. There is $B_0=B_0(\tau,\eta,C,A)$ such that, uniformly for $$\frac{x^\tau}{2}\le N\le x^\eta,\qquad q\le L^C,\qquad L^{B_0}\le \|t\|\le x^2,$$ every Dirichlet character $\chi$ modulo $q$ and every interval $I\subset[N,2N]$ satisfy $$\label{eq:long-prime} \left\|\sum_{p\in I}\chi(p)p^{-1+it}\right\| \ll_{\tau,\eta,C,A}L^{-A}.$$
  - Explanation: Character-twisted prime sums over long dyadic ranges cancel to an arbitrary negative power of the logarithm once the frequency is large enough.
- **claim-log-phase** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-log-phase)): Fix $C>0$. There is an absolute constant $C_3>0$ such that, for sufficiently large $x$ in terms of $C$, the following holds uniformly: $$\exp\!\left(\frac{L}{(\log L)^2}\right)\le N\le2x^5, \quad q\le L^C, \quad \exp\!\left(\frac{L}{2(\log L)^2}\right)\le \|t\|\le4x^3.$$ For every residue $a\pmod q$ and every interval $I\subset[N,2N]$, $$\label{eq:log-phase} \left\|\sum_{\substack{n\in I\\n\equiv a\pmod q}}n^{it}\right\| \ll \frac Nq\exp\!\left(-\frac{L}{(\log L)^{C_3}}\right).$$
  - Explanation: Sums of logarithmic phases over arithmetic progressions exhibit strong cancellation at high frequency.
- **claim-rough-counts** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rough-counts)): Fix $\delta>0$, $C>0$, $0<a<1$ and $A>0$. Suppose $x^\delta\le H\le x^C$ and $I\subset[H,2H]$ is an interval. Then $$\label{eq:rough-count} \#\{n\in I:P^-(n)>W\}=V(W)\|I\|+O(HL^{-A}).$$ If $d\le\exp(L^a)$ and every prime divisor of $d$ exceeds $W$, then for every residue $b\pmod d$, $$\label{eq:rough-off-sieve-progression} \#\{n\in I:n\equiv b\pmod d,\ P^-(n)>W\} =\frac{V(W)\|I\|}{d}+O\!\left(\frac Hd L^{-A}\right).$$ For every character $\chi$ modulo $q\le L^C$, $$\label{eq:rough-character} \sum_{\substack{n\in I\\P^-(n)>W}}\chi(n) =\mathbf{1}_{\chi\text{ principal}}V(W)\|I\|+O(HL^{-A}).$$ All statements are uniform in the indicated intervals and residues.
  - Explanation: Counts of integers free of small prime factors in long intervals, progressions and character sums follow the expected density with an arbitrarily good logarithmic error.
- **claim-block-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-block-sieve)): Consider finitely many objects with nonnegative weights and a bad condition at each of some designated primes $p\le z$. Suppose that the total weight on which all conditions indexed by the primes dividing a squarefree $d$ hold is $$Xg(d)+r(d),$$ including $d=1$, where $X\ge0$, $g$ is multiplicative, and fixed $\eta_0>0,C_0$ satisfy $$0\le g(p)\le1-\eta_0, \qquad \sum_{w<p\le w^2}g(p)\le C_0\quad(w>1).$$ For every sufficiently large even integer $h$, the weight avoiding all designated bad conditions is $$\label{eq:block-sieve} X\prod_{p\le z}(1-g(p))\bigl(1+O(e^{-h})\bigr) +O\!\left(\sum_{\substack{d\le z^{4h+2}\\d\text{ squarefree}}} \|r(d)\|\right).$$ Products and sums use only designated primes. Constants depend only on the density bounds and are uniform in $h$. For $h=2$, an upper bound holds with a fixed constant times the main term and the same remainder sum.
  - Explanation: A sieve for nonnegative weighted objects with bad conditions at designated primes, giving the expected main term and a remainder controlled by a squarefree sum.
- **claim-type-ii-marked** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-type-ii)): Fix $\delta,C,D_*>0$ and $q\in(0,1)$. Suppose $H_m,H_n\geq x^\delta$ and $X=H_mH_n\asymp x$. Let $F$ satisfy $\|F(h)\|\leq1$ and $F(ph)=F(h)$ for every prime in the groups <a href="#det:bands" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="det:bands">[det:bands]</a>. Let $\alpha_m$ be supported on an arbitrary interval in $[H_m,2H_m]$, where it has the value $$\alpha_m=m^{iv}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m\text{ prime}} -\frac{\mathbf{1}_{m\text{ rough}}}{V(W)\log m}\right), \qquad \|v\|\leq L^C.$$ Let $\beta_n$ be supported on rough integers in $[H_n,2H_n]$, with $\|\beta_n\|\leq L^C$. If $K$ is sufficiently large in terms of $\delta,C,D_*,q$, then $$\label{eq:type-II} \left\|\sum_{m,n}\alpha_m\beta_n F(mn-1)\mathcal W(mn-1)\right\| \ll X L^{-D_*}.$$ The required lower bound on $K$ is independent of the particular $a_i$. The implicit constant and the threshold for $x$ may depend on all the fixed parameters, including the $a_i$.
  - Explanation: A one-sided marked Type II estimate: the bilinear sum with divisor marks only on the predecessor side saves an arbitrary power of the logarithm.
- **claim-good-state-measure** (bound, [source](#stmt-det-good-state)): For any fixed lists, the set of $r\in\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$ failing goodness has Lebesgue measure at most $\exp(-cL^{0.1})$, for some $c>0$ and all sufficiently large $x$.
  - Explanation: The set of root ratios that fail the goodness tests is exponentially small in a power of the logarithm.
- **claim-root-residue** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-root-residue)): There is $c_\delta>0$ such that the following holds. Let $S$ be squarefree with $S\leq\exp(C_KL^{0.98})$, and prescribe $g_0\in\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb Z/S\mathbb Z)$. If $I_1,I_2,I_3$ are intervals in $[1,16],[1,2],[0,1]$, respectively, the number of primitive roots satisfying $$u/U\in I_1,\qquad v/V\in I_2,\qquad r\in I_3, \qquad g\equiv g_0\pmod S$$ is $$\label{det:root-residue-count} \frac{UV\,\|I_1\|\|I_2\|\|I_3\|} {\zeta(2)\|\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb Z/S\mathbb Z)\|} +O(UVx^{-c_\delta}).$$ The error is uniform in the intervals and in $g_0$.
  - Explanation: Primitive roots with prescribed matrix residues are equidistributed in boxes with a power saving.
- **claim-root-replacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-det-root-replacement)): In the normalized path expansion of <a href="#eq:det-moment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:det-moment">[eq:det-moment]</a>, one may replace the root average by $$\frac1{\zeta(2)}\int_{[1,16]\times[1,2]\times[0,1]} \,\mathrm d(u/U)\,\,\mathrm d(v/V)\,\,\mathrm dr$$ and by independent uniformly distributed lines in $\mathbb P^1(\mathbb F_p)$ for all the group primes. The real matrix at an archimedean root is $$g(u,v,r)=\begin{pmatrix}u&ur\\v&vr+1/u\end{pmatrix}.$$ All box and good-state conditions are retained. The total error is $O_A(L^{-AN})$ for every fixed $A>0$. The same assertion, with polynomial logarithmic coefficient bounds, holds for one edge and for failure of either endpoint’s goodness.
  - Explanation: The root average in the path expansion can be replaced by a continuous integral together with independent uniform projective lines at the group primes, at negligible cost.
- **claim-minor-arc-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-minor-moment)): For every fixed $E_0>0$, one can choose $A_0$ sufficiently large and then $K$ sufficiently large, in terms of $E_0,q$ and the fixed parameters of Theorem <a href="#thm:type-II" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:type-II">8</a>, so that the operator with the good-state projections satisfies $$\label{mem:moment-bound} \frac{1}{UV}\sum_{\substack{P\in\Omega\\P\ \mathrm{primitive}}} \langle \mathbf u_P,(\mathcal A\mathcal A^*)^R\mathbf u_P\rangle \le L^{-E_0N}.$$ The choices of $A_0,K$ do not depend on the particular fixed band exponents $a_1<\cdots<a_K$; the threshold for $x$ may depend on them. Consequently, for any prescribed fixed $A>0$, the determinant indicator in the expanded square of the preceding section can be replaced by <a href="#eq:det-major" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:det-major">[eq:det-major]</a>, with error $O_A(XY L^{-A})$. In the resulting major term the disjointness restriction on the two unshared products $a,b$ can be omitted. Equivalently, with the notation of <a href="#det:cauchy-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="det:cauchy-square">[det:cauchy-square]</a> and <a href="#maj:sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="maj:sum">[maj:sum]</a>, $$\label{mem:replacement-interface} \abs{\mathcal Q_Y-\mathcal Q_Y^{\mathrm{maj}}} \ll_A XY L^{-A}.$$ The precision needed in the pairing estimate <a href="#eq:det-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:det-pairing">[eq:det-pairing]</a> for this consequence is independent of $K$.
  - Explanation: The trace moment of the good-state operator with memory decays like a large power of the logarithm per edge, which lets the determinant indicator be replaced by its major-arc form.
- **claim-major-term** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-major-term)): For every fixed $D>0$, the unrestricted major sum $$\label{maj:sum} \begin{split} \mathcal Q_Y^{\mathrm{maj}}={}& \left(\prod_{i=1}^K V_i^{-2}\right) \sum_{a,b,m,n,r,s} \eta(a/Y)\eta(b/Y)\alpha_m\overline{\alpha_r} \beta_n\overline{\beta_s}\, \mathcal H_{\mathfrak M}(bmn-ars;a,b) \end{split}$$ satisfies $$\label{maj:bound} \|\mathcal Q_Y^{\mathrm{maj}}\|\ll_D XYL^{-D}.$$ Here $a,b$ run independently over products of one prime from each $\mathcal P_i$, with no disjointness condition, and $\mathcal H_{\mathfrak M}$ denotes the kernel in <a href="#eq:det-major" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:det-major">[eq:det-major]</a>. The estimate is uniform in the coefficient intervals, in $\|v\|\le L^C$, and in the admissible scale $Y$.
  - Explanation: The unrestricted determinant major sum is smaller than every fixed negative power of the logarithm times the natural size.
- **claim-long-coefficient** (bound, [source](#stmt-maj-long-coefficient)): Fix $A_0,B,A>0$. For every character $\chi$ of modulus at most $L^{A_0}$, put $$\label{maj:M-definition} M_\alpha(t)=\frac1{H_m}\sum_m\alpha_m\chi(m)m^{it}.$$ Then, uniformly for $\|t\|\le 2XL^B$, $$\label{maj:M-uniform} \|M_\alpha(t)\|\ll_A L^{-A}.$$ The assertion also holds with conjugated coefficients and characters.
  - Explanation: The centered prime-minus-rough coefficient polynomial is uniformly small up to large frequencies.
- **claim-sparse-frequency** (bound, [source](#stmt-maj-sparse-frequency)): Let $P$ satisfy $cL^{0.1}\le\log P\le C_1L^{0.2}$, and let $\mathcal P$ be any set of primes in $[P,2P]$. For any real $\xi$ and any character $\chi$, put $$P_s(t)=P^{-1}\sum_{p\in\mathcal P}\chi(p)p^{i(t+\xi)}.$$ Let $N_\beta(t)=H_n^{-1}\sum_n\beta_n\chi_n(n)n^{it}$, with $\|\beta_n\|\le L^C$ on $[H_n,2H_n]$ and $x^\delta\le H_n\ll x$. For every fixed $B,A_s>0$, the unit intervals meeting $$\{t:\|t\|\le 2XL^B,\ \|P_s(t)\|>L^{-A_s}\}$$ form a family $\mathcal I$ satisfying $$\label{maj:sparse-count} \|\mathcal I\|\le\exp(O(L^{0.91})), \qquad \sum_{I\in\mathcal I}\sup_{t\in I}\|N_\beta(t)\|^2 \ll L^{2C+1}.$$ Both assertions are uniform in $\xi$ and in the prime set.
  - Explanation: Times where a small-prime polynomial is not small lie in a sparse family of unit intervals, on which the other polynomial has bounded energy.
- **claim-two-sided-correlation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-two-sided)): Fix $d\geq1$, $\varepsilon>0$, and intervals $I_1,\ldots,I_d$ whose lower endpoints are at least $x^\varepsilon$ and whose upper endpoints have product at most $x^{1-\varepsilon}$. Let $\mathbf l=(l_1,\ldots,l_d)$ run over ordered tuples of distinct primes with $l_i\in I_i$, and put $$\label{cor:centered-core} l:=l_1\cdots l_d,\qquad C_x:=\sum_{\mathbf l}\frac1l,\qquad F_x(n):=\mathcal W(n) \left(\sum_{\mathbf l}\mathbf{1}_{l\mid n}-C_x\right).$$ Suppose $G(n)=G(n_*)$ and $\|G(n)\|\leq L^C\tau(n_*)^C$, for fixed $C$. For every fixed smooth compactly supported $\Psi:(0,\infty)\to\mathbb C$ and every fixed $A>0$, $$\label{eq:two-sided} \sum_{w\geq1}\frac{\Psi(w/x)}w F_x(w)G(w+1)W_1(w)W_1(w+1)\ll_A L^{-A}.$$ The constants and sufficiently-large-$x$ threshold may depend on all the fixed data, including the fixed inert bands, but the estimate is uniform over the active groups and slot intervals satisfying the stated bounds. The slots have no joint restriction other than distinctness.
  - Explanation: A two-sided marked correlation of the centered long-prime divisor statistic with a rough-supported weight saves an arbitrary power of the logarithm.
- **claim-graph-transference** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-graph-transfer)): Assume $\sum_\nu\sup\|K_\nu\|\leq L^{C_1}$, where $m_0,C_1$ are fixed independently of $J$. For every fixed $E>0$ there is $E_{\rm id}>0$, depending only on $E$ and the fixed group data, such that $$\sup_{\Theta\in\mathbb R} \\|\mathcal T_\zeta(\Theta)\\|_{2\to2}\leq L^{-E_{\rm id}}$$ has the following consequence for all sufficiently large fixed $J$. Let $I=[X,2X)$, with $x^\gamma\leq X\leq x^{1/\gamma}$ for fixed $\gamma>0$. If $f$ is supported on positions in $I$, is independent of the chosen marks, and satisfies $$\sup\|f\|\leq\exp(O(\sqrt L)),\qquad \\|f\\|,\\|g\\|\leq X^{1/2}L^{C_3},$$ then $$\label{cor:transferred-pairing} \|\langle f,\mathcal A_\zeta g\rangle\| \ll XL^{-E+2C_3}.$$ The lower bound on $J$ may also depend on $m_0,C_1$. The threshold for $x$ may depend on $J,C_2,\gamma$. No further restriction on $\zeta$ is imposed beyond the ideal norm hypothesis. Taking absolute values of the individual transition coefficients gives row and column sums at most $L^{C_{\rm abs}}$, where $C_{\rm abs}$ is independent of $J$.
  - Explanation: An ideal-space norm bound transfers to a physical endpoint pairing bound for the dilation graph operator, with absolute row and column sums controlled independently of the padding.
- **claim-ideal-family** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-ideal-family)): For every $E_{\rm id}>0$ and fixed $C_4\geq0$, there are fixed $m_0,J_0,C_1$ such that, for each fixed $J\geq J_0$, a family consisting of the raw pattern $C_g=\varnothing$, $K_0=1$, and signed comparison patterns satisfies $$\label{cor:ideal-bound} \sum_\nu\sup\|K_\nu\|\leq L^{C_1},\qquad \sup_{\substack{\Theta\in\mathbb R\\\|\zeta\|\leq L^{C_4}}} \\|\mathcal T_\zeta(\Theta)\\|_{2\to2}\leq L^{-E_{\rm id}}.$$ The family is independent of $\Theta,\zeta$; its defining parameters are independent of $J$.* *Split the big groups into two blocks. Every comparison frees nonempty sets of probes $A,B$ in the respective blocks, with coefficient $$\label{cor:comparison-kernel} -(-1)^{\|A\|+\|B\|}\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B).$$ Here $D_A,D_B$ are the free products, $X_A$ is the corresponding target-mark product, and $X_B$ the corresponding source-mark product. No shared or final $\ell$ label enters these kernels. For a nonempty slot set $\Lambda$ with at most $m_0$ slots in each group, $$\label{cor:cell-kernel} \mathcal K_\Lambda(D,X) =\sum_{j:\nu_{\Lambda j}\geq\xi} \frac{\mathbf{1}_{\log D\in I_j}\mathbf{1}_{\log X\in I_j}}{\nu_{\Lambda j}} \sum_{\substack{\chi\text{ primitive}\\\mathop{\mathrm{cond}}(\chi)\leq Q}} \overline{\chi(D)}\chi(X), \qquad I_j=[jh,(j+1)h).$$ The number $\nu_{\Lambda j}$ is the cell probability for the independent slot laws. The parameters $Q,h^{-1},\xi^{-1}$ are fixed powers of $L$, with $h\leq1$. There are at most $Q^2$ characters and $O_{m_0}(1+L^{d_0}\log L/h)$ nonempty cells, and $$\sup\|\mathcal K_\Lambda\|\leq Q^2\xi^{-1},\qquad \sup_X\mathbb E_D\|\mathcal K_\Lambda(D,X)\|\leq Q^2.$$ More precisely, for any prescribed fixed $A_0>0$, the parameters may be chosen, independently of $J$, so that for arbitrary $L^2$ tuple tests $f(X_B),g(X_A)$ and all $\theta\in\mathbb R$, $\|\zeta\|\leq L^{C_4}$, $$\begin{aligned} \big\|\mathbb E\,\overline{f(X_B)}g(X_A) \big[&(X_AX_B)^{i\zeta}\mathrm{e}(\theta X_AX_B)\notag\\ &-\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B) (D_AD_B)^{i\zeta}\mathrm{e}(\theta D_AD_B)\big]\big\| \leq L^{-A_0}\\|f\\|_2\\|g\\|_2. \label{cor:comparison-contract} \end{aligned}$$ The four label tuples in this expectation are independent; the tests need not depend only on their products. Expanding the two kernels, including all patterns, has total coefficient mass and number of terms bounded by fixed powers of $L$.
  - Explanation: A family consisting of the raw pattern and signed comparison patterns achieves the ideal norm bound with polynomially bounded total coefficient mass.
- **claim-local-mellin** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-mellin)): Let $a_w$ be supported on $w\asymp Y$, where $Y=x^{1+o(1)}$, and suppose $H=x^{o(1)}\to\infty$. For every fixed integer $j\geq1$, $$\label{cor:local-mellin} \int_{\|\beta\|\leq H^{-1}}\|\widehat a(\beta)\|^2\,\,\mathrm d\beta \ll_j\frac1Y\int_{\mathbb R}(1+H\|t\|/Y)^{-j} \left\|\sum_w a_ww^{it}\right\|^2\,\,\mathrm dt +\left(\frac HY\right)^2\sum_w\|a_w\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Energy of an additive Fourier transform near zero is controlled by a weighted Mellin-type integral plus a small diagonal term.
- **claim-divisor-model** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-divisor-model)): Let $H_{\rm marks}(n)=\mathcal W(n)W_{\mathbf t}^{\mathbf b}(n)$, where $0\leq t_g\leq m'$ for fixed $m'$ and $\|\mathbf b\|\leq1$. In particular, zero values of $t_g$ are allowed; these occur when a small mark is removed below. There is a divisor sum $$\label{cor:divisor-model} H_0(n)=\sum_{d'\mid n}A_{d'},\qquad d'\leq\exp(O(L^{.47})),\qquad \sum_{d'}\frac{\|A_{d'}\|}{d'}\leq L^{C_8},$$ whose prime divisors all belong to the active or inert groups, such that, on every positive integer dyad $n\asymp R$, $$\label{cor:model-error} \sum_{n\asymp R}\|H_{\rm marks}(n)-H_0(n)\|^2 \ll_N R L^{-N}$$ for every fixed $N$. The assertion is uniform in the bounded tuple test $\mathbf b$, with no regularity assumption on that test.
  - Explanation: The marked weights are approximated in mean square by a truncated divisor sum with controlled reciprocal coefficient mass.
- **claim-interior-statistic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-interior)): For every nonnegative $\Phi\in C_c^\infty((0,\infty))$, $$\label{eq:interior} \sum_{p\text{ prime}}\Phi((p-1)/x) \bigl(f_x(p-1)-C_x\bigr)=o(x/\log x).$$ The limit holds through all real $x\to\infty$ for the slot intervals just described.
  - Explanation: The centered prime divisor statistic has a negligible smooth average over primes, which gives interior factorial moment asymptotics.
- **claim-summed-progressions** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-progressions)): Choose $0<c_0<\varepsilon/2$. Let $H$ be a weight from a fixed finite collection of disjoint arrays, a product of two such weights (allowing a repeated array), a constant, or a bounded linear combination of these functions. Then, for every fixed $A>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \label{ext:progression} \sum_{r\leq x^{c_0}}\left\| \sum_{\substack{u\geq1\\r\mid u+1}} \Phi(u/x)f_x(u)H(u) -\frac{x}{r}\left(\int_0^\infty\Phi(t)\,\,\mathrm dt\right) C_x M_x(H)\right\| &\ll_A xL^{-A}. \end{aligned}$$ The same assertion holds with $f_x,C_x$ replaced by $1,1$. The constants may depend on the fixed arrays and on the bounded coefficients in the linear combination.
  - Explanation: Summed progression remainders for the divisor statistic times marking weights match the independent divisor model up to a logarithmic saving.
- **claim-prime-polynomial** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-prime-polynomial)): Fix $0<\tau<\eta<1$, $C>0$, and $A>0$. There is a constant $B_0=B_0(\tau,\eta,C,A)$ such that, uniformly for $$\frac{x^\tau}{2}\le N\le x^\eta,\qquad q\le L^C,\qquad L^{B_0}\le \|t\|\le x^2,$$ every Dirichlet character $\chi$ modulo $q$ and every interval $I\subset[N,2N]$ satisfy $$\label{lp:prime-estimate} \left\|\sum_{p\in I}\chi(p)p^{-1+it}\right\| \ll_{\tau,\eta,C,A} L^{-A}.$$
  - Explanation: Appendix proof of the long prime polynomial estimate by Mellin inversion in a zero-free strip.
- **claim-pre-primes** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-pre-primes)): There is an absolute constant $A_1>0$ such that, for every integer $k\ge2$ and every real $y\ge A_1k^6$, the interval $[y,2y]$ contains at least $2k^3+5$ primes, all greater than $k$.
  - Explanation: Intervals of sufficient length contain many primes larger than the degree, as needed for the congruence iteration.
- **claim-monotone-derivative** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-pre-derivatives)): Let $f$ be real-valued and continuously differentiable on an interval. Suppose $f'$ is monotone and takes values in $[j+\lambda,j+1-\lambda]$ for an integer $j$ and $0<\lambda\le1/2$. Then, on every subinterval, $$\left\|\sum_n\mathrm{e}(f(n))\right\|\ll\lambda^{-1},$$ where the sum is over the integers in that subinterval and the implied constant is absolute.
  - Explanation: A first-derivative test for exponential sums whose derivative is monotone and stays away from the integers.
- **claim-power-sum-mean-value** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-mean-value)): There are absolute constants $C_1,C_2>0$ such that, for every integer $k\ge2$, some integer $s$ with $k\le s\le C_1k^4$ satisfies $$\label{lp:mean-value-bound} J_{s,k}(M)\le \exp(C_1k^{C_2})M^{2s-K+1/100} \qquad(M\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: A degree-uniform quantitative Vinogradov-type mean value bound obtained by a congruence iteration.
- **claim-log-phase-appendix** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-log-phase)): Fix $C>0$. There is an absolute constant $C_3>0$ such that, for sufficiently large $x$ in terms of $C$, the following holds uniformly: $$\exp(L/T^2)\le N'\le2x^5,\qquad q\le L^C,\qquad \exp(L/(2T^2))\le \|v\|\le4x^3.$$ For every residue $a\pmod q$ and every interval $I\subset[N',2N']$, $$\label{lp:progression-estimate} \left\|\sum_{\substack{n\in I\\n\equiv a\pmod q}}n^{iv}\right\| \ll \frac{N'}q\exp(-L/T^{C_3}).$$
  - Explanation: Appendix proof of cancellation of logarithmic phases on progressions using the power-sum bound.
- **claim-l-function-upper-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-l-upper)): Fix $C>0$ and put $r_*=T^4/L$. Uniformly for $q\le L^C$, $$\label{lp:L-upper-bound} \log\|D(\sigma+iv,\chi)\|\ll_C T^2 \quad\left(\|\sigma-1\|\le10r_*,\quad \frac12\le\|v\|\le3x^3\right).$$
  - Explanation: Dirichlet L-functions have a controlled logarithmic size near the line of absolute convergence up to a large height.
- **claim-local-log-derivative** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-local-log-derivative)): Fix $C>0$, let $q\le L^C$, and put $s_0=1+r_*+iv$, where $1\le\|v\|\le\tfrac52x^3$. There is a radius $R$ with $4r_*\le R\le5r_*$, with no zero on its boundary, for which $$\label{lp:local-log-derivative-formula} \frac{D'}{D}(s,\chi) =\sum_{\|\rho-s_0\|<R}\frac1{s-\rho} +O_C(T^2/r_*)\qquad(\|s-s_0\|\le2r_*),$$ away from zeros. The sum counts zeros with multiplicity and has $O_C(T^2)$ terms.
  - Explanation: The logarithmic derivative is a sum over nearby zeros plus a controlled error.
- **claim-zero-free-strip** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lp-zero-free)): For each fixed $C>0$ there is $c=c(C)>0$ such that every character of modulus at most $L^C$ has no zero in $$\label{lp:zero-free-region} \operatorname{Re}s\ge1-cT^2/L,\qquad 1\le\|\operatorname{Im}s\|\le x^3.$$ Moreover, $$\label{lp:log-derivative-bound} \left\|\frac{D'}D(s,\chi)\right\|\ll_C L \quad\left(1-\frac{cT^2}{2L}\le\operatorname{Re}s\le1+\frac1L, \quad2\le\|\operatorname{Im}s\|\le\frac{x^3}{2}\right).$$
  - Explanation: A zero-free strip at large height and a bound on the logarithmic derivative inside it.

      "exact_statement": "For every fixed $k\\ge1$ and every bounded continuous function $F:[0,1]^k\\to\\mathbb R$, $$\\label{eq:main-law}\n \\lim_{x\\to\\infty}\\frac1{\\pi(x)-1}\n     \\sum_{\\substack{3\\le p\\le x\\\\p\\text{ prime}}}\n       F\\bigl(V_1(p),\\ldots,V_k(p)\\bigr)\n =\\mathbb EF(L_1,\\ldots,L_k).$$ The limit holds through all real $x$ and uses ordinary equal weighting of the primes.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "The prime number theorem holds with an error smaller than every fixed negative power of the logarithm. In particular, as $y\\to\\infty$, $$\\sum_{p\\le y}\\frac1p=\\log\\log y+c_M+o(1),\n \\qquad\n \\prod_{p\\le y}\\left(1-\\frac1p\\right)\n       \\sim\\frac{e^{-\\gamma_E}}{\\log y},$$ where $c_M$ and $\\gamma_E$ are constants. For fixed $A,C>0$, uniformly for $r\\le(\\log y)^C$ and $(a,r)=1$, $$\\#\\{p\\le y:p\\equiv a\\pmod r\\}\n =\\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Li}}(y)}{\\varphi(r)}\n     +O_{A,C}\\!\\left(\\frac{y}{(\\log y)^A}\\right).$$ The constants need not be effective.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-moment-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed nonnegative integer $k$ there is $C_k$ such that $$\\sum_{n\\le Y}\\tau(n)^k\\ll_k Y(\\log(2Y))^{C_k},\\qquad\n \\sum_{n\\le Y}\\frac{\\tau(n)^k}{n}\n       \\ll_k(\\log(2Y))^{C_k}.$$ Let $P(t)=\\sum_{n\\le Y}c_n n^{it}$. On every real interval $I$ of length $T\\ge0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_I|P(t)|^2\\,\\mathrm dt\n &\\ll (T+Y\\log(2Y))\\sum_n|c_n|^2,\\label{eq:dirichlet-mean}\\\\\n \\sum_{t\\in\\mathcal T}|P(t)|^2\n &\\ll (T+1+Y\\log(2Y))(\\log(2Y))^2\n                  \\sum_n|c_n|^2,\\label{eq:dirichlet-spaced}\n\\end{aligned}$$ whenever $\\mathcal T\\subset I$ has mutual spacing at least one.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-log-phase",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $C>0$. There is an absolute constant $C_3>0$ such that, for sufficiently large $x$ in terms of $C$, the following holds uniformly: $$\\exp\\!\\left(\\frac{L}{(\\log L)^2}\\right)\\le N\\le2x^5,\n \\quad q\\le L^C,\n \\quad\n \\exp\\!\\left(\\frac{L}{2(\\log L)^2}\\right)\\le |t|\\le4x^3.$$ For every residue $a\\pmod q$ and every interval $I\\subset[N,2N]$, $$\\label{eq:log-phase}\n \\left|\\sum_{\\substack{n\\in I\\\\n\\equiv a\\pmod q}}n^{it}\\right|\n \\ll \\frac Nq\\exp\\!\\left(-\\frac{L}{(\\log L)^{C_3}}\\right).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rough-counts",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\delta>0$, $C>0$, $0<a<1$ and $A>0$. Suppose $x^\\delta\\le H\\le x^C$ and $I\\subset[H,2H]$ is an interval. Then $$\\label{eq:rough-count}\n \\#\\{n\\in I:P^-(n)>W\\}=V(W)|I|+O(HL^{-A}).$$ If $d\\le\\exp(L^a)$ and every prime divisor of $d$ exceeds $W$, then for every residue $b\\pmod d$, $$\\label{eq:rough-off-sieve-progression}\n \\#\\{n\\in I:n\\equiv b\\pmod d,\\ P^-(n)>W\\}\n =\\frac{V(W)|I|}{d}+O\\!\\left(\\frac Hd L^{-A}\\right).$$ For every character $\\chi$ modulo $q\\le L^C$, $$\\label{eq:rough-character}\n \\sum_{\\substack{n\\in I\\\\P^-(n)>W}}\\chi(n)\n =\\mathbf{1}_{\\chi\\text{ principal}}V(W)|I|+O(HL^{-A}).$$ All statements are uniform in the indicated intervals and residues.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rough-counts",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $\\delta>0$, $C>0$, $0<a<1$ and $A>0$. Suppose $x^\\delta\\le H\\le x^C$ and $I\\subset[H,2H]$ is an interval.           "source_id": "PM-011.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix $\\delta>0$, $C>0$, $0<a<1$ and $A>0$. Suppose $x^\\delta\\le H\\le x^C$ and $I\\subset[H,2H]$ is an interval. Then $$\\label{eq:rough-count}\n \\#\\{n\\in I:P^-(n)>W\\}=V(W)|I|+O(HL^{-A}).$$ If $d\\le\\exp(L^a)$ and every prime divisor of $d$ exceeds $W$, then for every residue $b\\pmod d$, $$\\label{eq:rough-off-sieve-progression}\n \\#\\{n\\in I:n\\equiv b\\pmod d,\\ P^-(n)>W\\}\n =\\frac{V(W)|I|}{d}+O\\!\\left(\\frac Hd L^{-A}\\right).$$ For every character $\\chi$ modulo $q\\le L^C$, $$\\label{eq:rough-character}\n \\sum_{\\substack{n\\in I\\\\P^-(n)>W}}\\chi(n)\n =\\mathbf{1}_{\\chi\\text{ principal}}V(W)|I|+O(HL^{-A}).$$ All statements are uniform in the indicated intervals and residues.",

        "exact_quote": "Consider finitely many objects with nonnegative weights and a bad condition at each of some designated primes $p\\le z$. Suppose that the total weight on which all conditions indexed by the primes dividing a squarefree $d$ hold is $$Xg(d)+r(d),$$ including $d=1$, where $X\\ge0$, $g$ is multiplicative, and fixed $\\eta_0>0,C_0$ satisfy $$0\\le g(p)\\le1-\\eta_0,\n \\qquad \\sum_{w<p\\le w^2}g(p)\\le C_0\\quad(w>1).$$ For every sufficiently large even integer $h$, the weight avoiding all designated bad conditions is $$\\label{eq:block-sieve}\n X\\prod_{p\\le z}(1-g(p))\\bigl(1+O(e^{-h})\\bigr)\n +O\\!\\left(\\sum_{\\substack{d\\le z^{4h+2}\\\\d\\text{ squarefree}}}\n          |r(d)|\\right).$$ Products and sums use only designated primes.       },

        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\delta,C,D_*>0$ and $q\\in(0,1)$. Suppose $H_m,H_n\\geq x^\\delta$ and $X=H_mH_n\\asymp x$. Let $\\alpha_m$ be supported on an arbitrary interval in $[H_m,2H_m]$, where it has the value $$\\alpha_m=m^{iv}\\left(\\mathbf{1}_{m\\text{ prime}}\n       -\\frac{\\mathbf{1}_{m\\text{ rough}}}{V(W)\\log m}\\right),\n \\qquad |v|\\leq L^C.$$ Let $\\beta_n$ be supported on rough integers in $[H_n,2H_n]$, with $|\\beta_n|\\leq L^C$. If $K$ is sufficiently large in terms of $\\delta,C,D_*,q$, then $$\\label{eq:type-II}\n \\left|\\sum_{m,n}\\alpha_m\\beta_n F(mn-1)\\mathcal W(mn-1)\\right|\n       \\ll X L^{-D_*}.$$ The required lower bound on $K$ is independent of the particular $a_i$. Let $\\alpha_m$ be supported on an arbitrary interval in $[H_m,2H_m]$, where it has the value $$\\alpha_m=m^{iv}\\left(\\mathbf{1}_{m\\text{ prime}}\n       -\\frac{\\mathbf{1}_{m\\text{ rough}}}{V(W)\\log m}\\right),\n \\qquad |v|\\leq L^C.$$ Let $\\beta_n$ be supported on rough integers in $[H_n,2H_n]$, with $|\\beta_n|\\leq L^C$. If $K$ is sufficiently large in terms of $\\delta,C,D_*,q$, then $$\\label{eq:type-II}\n \\left|\\sum_{m,n}\\alpha_m\\beta_n F(mn-1)\\mathcal W(mn-1)\\right|\n       \\ll X L^{-D_*}.$$ The required lower bound on $K$ is independent of the particular $a_i$. The implicit constant and the threshold for $x$ may depend on all the fixed parameters, including the $a_i$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-root-residue",
        "exact_quote": "There is $c_\\delta>0$ such that the following holds. Let $S$ be squarefree with $S\\leq\\exp(C_KL^{0.98})$, and prescribe $g_0\\in\\mathrm{SL}_2(\\mathbb Z/S\\mathbb Z)$. If $I_1,I_2,I_3$ are intervals in $[1,16],[1,2],[0,1]$, respectively, the number of primitive roots satisfying $$u/U\\in I_1,\\qquad v/V\\in I_2,\\qquad r\\in I_3,\n \\qquad g\\equiv g_0\\pmod S$$ is $$\\label{det:root-residue-count}\n \\frac{UV\\,|I_1||I_2||I_3|}\n      {\\zeta(2)|\\mathrm{SL}_2(\\mathbb Z/S\\mathbb Z)|}\n             +O(UVx^{-c_\\delta}).$$ The error is uniform in the intervals and in $g_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-root-residue",
          "exact_quote": "There is $c_\\delta>0$ such that the following holds. Let $S$ be squarefree with $S\\leq\\exp(C_KL^{0.98})$, and prescribe $g_0\\in\\mathrm{SL}_2(\\mathbb Z/S\\mathbb Z)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-det-root-replacement",
        "exact_quote": "In the normalized path expansion of <a href=\"#eq:det-moment\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:det-moment\">[eq:det-moment]</a>, one may replace the root average by $$\\frac1{\\zeta(2)}\\int_{[1,16]\\times[1,2]\\times[0,1]}\n                  \\,\\mathrm d(u/U)\\,\\,\\mathrm d(v/V)\\,\\,\\mathrm dr$$ and by independent uniformly distributed lines in $\\mathbb P^1(\\mathbb F_p)$ for all the group primes. The real matrix at an archimedean root is $$g(u,v,r)=\\begin{pmatrix}u&ur\\\\v&vr+1/u\\end{pmatrix}.$$ All box and good-state conditions are retained.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-minor-moment",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $E_0>0$, one can choose $A_0$ sufficiently large and then $K$ sufficiently large, in terms of $E_0,q$ and the fixed parameters of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:type-II\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:type-II\">8</a>, so that the operator with the good-state projections satisfies $$\\label{mem:moment-bound}\n \\frac{1}{UV}\\sum_{\\substack{P\\in\\Omega\\\\P\\ \\mathrm{primitive}}}\n \\langle \\mathbf u_P,(\\mathcal A\\mathcal A^*)^R\\mathbf u_P\\rangle\n \\le L^{-E_0N}.$$ The choices of $A_0,K$ do not depend on the particular fixed band exponents $a_1<\\cdots<a_K$; the threshold for $x$ may depend on them. Consequently, for any prescribed fixed $A>0$, the determinant indicator in the expanded square of the preceding section can be replaced by <a href=\"#eq:det-major\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:det-major\">[eq:det-major]</a>, with error $O_A(XY L^{-A})$. In the resulting major term the disjointness restriction on the two unshared products $a,b$ can be omitted. Equivalently, with the notation of <a href=\"#det:cauchy-square\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"det:cauchy-square\">[det:cauchy-square]</a> and <a href=\"#maj:sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"maj:sum\">[maj:sum]</a>, $$\\label{mem:replacement-interface}\n \\abs{\\mathcal Q_Y-\\mathcal Q_Y^{\\mathrm{maj}}}\n      \\ll_A XY L^{-A}.$$ The precision needed in the pairing estimate <a href=\"#eq:det-pairing\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:det-pairing\">[eq:det-pairing]</a> for this consequence is independent of $K$.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-minor-moment",

Consequently, for any prescribed fixed $A>0$, the determinant indicator in the expanded square of the preceding section can be replaced by <a href=\"#eq:det-major\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:det-major\">[eq:det-major]</a>, with error $O_A(XY L^{-A})$. In the resulting major term the disjointness restriction on the two unshared products $a,b$ can be omitted. Equivalently, with the notation of <a href=\"#det:cauchy-square\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"det:cauchy-square\">[det:cauchy-square]</a> and <a href=\"#maj:sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"maj:sum\">[maj:sum]</a>, $$\\label{mem:replacement-interface}\n \\abs{\\mathcal Q_Y-\\mathcal Q_Y^{\\mathrm{maj}}}\n      \\ll_A XY L^{-A}.$$ The precision needed in the pairing estimate <a href=\"#eq:det-pairing\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:det-pairing\">[eq:det-pairing]</a> for this consequence is independent of $K$.",

      "exact_statement": "For every fixed $E_0>0$, one can choose $A_0$ sufficiently large and then $K$ sufficiently large, in terms of $E_0,q$ and the fixed parameters of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:type-II\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:type-II\">8</a>, so that the operator with the good-state projections satisfies $$\\label{mem:moment-bound}\n \\frac{1}{UV}\\sum_{\\substack{P\\in\\Omega\\\\P\\ \\mathrm{primitive}}}\n \\langle \\mathbf u_P,(\\mathcal A\\mathcal A^*)^R\\mathbf u_P\\rangle\n \\le L^{-E_0N}.$$ The choices of $A_0,K$ do not depend on the particular fixed band exponents $a_1<\\cdots<a_K$; the threshold for $x$ may depend on them.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-maj-sparse-frequency",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ satisfy $cL^{0.1}\\le\\log P\\le C_1L^{0.2}$, and let $\\mathcal P$ be any set of primes in $[P,2P]$. For any real $\\xi$ and any character $\\chi$, put $$P_s(t)=P^{-1}\\sum_{p\\in\\mathcal P}\\chi(p)p^{i(t+\\xi)}.$$ Let $N_\\beta(t)=H_n^{-1}\\sum_n\\beta_n\\chi_n(n)n^{it}$, with $|\\beta_n|\\le L^C$ on $[H_n,2H_n]$ and $x^\\delta\\le H_n\\ll x$. For every fixed $B,A_s>0$, the unit intervals meeting $$\\{t:|t|\\le 2XL^B,\\ |P_s(t)|>L^{-A_s}\\}$$ form a family $\\mathcal I$ satisfying $$\\label{maj:sparse-count}\n |\\mathcal I|\\le\\exp(O(L^{0.91})),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal I}\\sup_{t\\in I}|N_\\beta(t)|^2\n       \\ll L^{2C+1}.$$ Both assertions are uniform in $\\xi$ and in the prime set.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-maj-sparse-frequency",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P$ satisfy $cL^{0.1}\\le\\log P\\le C_1L^{0.2}$, and let $\\mathcal P$ be any set of primes in $[P,2P]$. For every fixed $B,A_s>0$, the unit intervals meeting $$\\{t:|t|\\le 2XL^B,\\ |P_s(t)|>L^{-A_s}\\}$$ form a family $\\mathcal I$ satisfying $$\\label{maj:sparse-count}\n |\\mathcal I|\\le\\exp(O(L^{0.91})),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal I}\\sup_{t\\in I}|N_\\beta(t)|^2\n       \\ll L^{2C+1}.$$ Both assertions are uniform in $\\xi$ and in the prime set.",
          "source_id": "PM-011.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $P$ satisfy $cL^{0.1}\\le\\log P\\le C_1L^{0.2}$, and let $\\mathcal P$ be any set of primes in $[P,2P]$. For any real $\\xi$ and any character $\\chi$, put $$P_s(t)=P^{-1}\\sum_{p\\in\\mathcal P}\\chi(p)p^{i(t+\\xi)}.$$ Let $N_\\beta(t)=H_n^{-1}\\sum_n\\beta_n\\chi_n(n)n^{it}$, with $|\\beta_n|\\le L^C$ on $[H_n,2H_n]$ and $x^\\delta\\le H_n\\ll x$. For every fixed $B,A_s>0$, the unit intervals meeting $$\\{t:|t|\\le 2XL^B,\\ |P_s(t)|>L^{-A_s}\\}$$ form a family $\\mathcal I$ satisfying $$\\label{maj:sparse-count}\n |\\mathcal I|\\le\\exp(O(L^{0.91})),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal I}\\sup_{t\\in I}|N_\\beta(t)|^2\n       \\ll L^{2C+1}.$$ Both assertions are uniform in $\\xi$ and in the prime set.",

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-two-sided",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\geq1$, $\\varepsilon>0$, and intervals $I_1,\\ldots,I_d$ whose lower endpoints are at least $x^\\varepsilon$ and whose upper endpoints have product at most $x^{1-\\varepsilon}$. Let $\\mathbf l=(l_1,\\ldots,l_d)$ run over ordered tuples of distinct primes with $l_i\\in I_i$, and put $$\\label{cor:centered-core}\n l:=l_1\\cdots l_d,\\qquad\n C_x:=\\sum_{\\mathbf l}\\frac1l,\\qquad\n F_x(n):=\\mathcal W(n)\n       \\left(\\sum_{\\mathbf l}\\mathbf{1}_{l\\mid n}-C_x\\right).$$ Suppose $G(n)=G(n_*)$ and $|G(n)|\\leq L^C\\tau(n_*)^C$, for fixed $C$. For every fixed smooth compactly supported $\\Psi:(0,\\infty)\\to\\mathbb C$ and every fixed $A>0$, $$\\label{eq:two-sided}\n \\sum_{w\\geq1}\\frac{\\Psi(w/x)}w\n       F_x(w)G(w+1)W_1(w)W_1(w+1)\\ll_A L^{-A}.$$ The constants and sufficiently-large-$x$ threshold may depend on all the fixed data, including the fixed inert bands, but the estimate is uniform over the active groups and slot intervals satisfying the stated bounds. The slots have no joint restriction other than distinctness.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-graph-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $\\sum_\\nu\\sup|K_\\nu|\\leq L^{C_1}$, where $m_0,C_1$ are fixed independently of $J$. For every fixed $E>0$ there is $E_{\\rm id}>0$, depending only on $E$ and the fixed group data, such that $$\\sup_{\\Theta\\in\\mathbb R}\n    \\|\\mathcal T_\\zeta(\\Theta)\\|_{2\\to2}\\leq L^{-E_{\\rm id}}$$ has the following consequence for all sufficiently large fixed $J$. Let $I=[X,2X)$, with $x^\\gamma\\leq X\\leq x^{1/\\gamma}$ for fixed $\\gamma>0$. If $f$ is supported on positions in $I$, is independent of the chosen marks, and satisfies $$\\sup|f|\\leq\\exp(O(\\sqrt L)),\\qquad\n \\|f\\|,\\|g\\|\\leq X^{1/2}L^{C_3},$$ then $$\\label{cor:transferred-pairing}\n |\\langle f,\\mathcal A_\\zeta g\\rangle|\n       \\ll XL^{-E+2C_3}.$$ The lower bound on $J$ may also depend on $m_0,C_1$. The threshold for $x$ may depend on $J,C_2,\\gamma$. No further restriction on $\\zeta$ is imposed beyond the ideal norm hypothesis. Taking absolute values of the individual transition coefficients gives row and column sums at most $L^{C_{\\rm abs}}$, where $C_{\\rm abs}$ is independent of $J$.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "explanation": "An ideal-space norm bound transfers to a physical endpoint pairing bound for the dilation graph operator, with absolute row and column sums controlled independently of the padding.",

      "id": "claim-graph-transference",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-ideal-family",
        "exact_quote": "For every $E_{\\rm id}>0$ and fixed $C_4\\geq0$, there are fixed $m_0,J_0,C_1$ such that, for each fixed $J\\geq J_0$, a family consisting of the raw pattern $C_g=\\varnothing$, $K_0=1$, and signed comparison patterns satisfies $$\\label{cor:ideal-bound}\n \\sum_\\nu\\sup|K_\\nu|\\leq L^{C_1},\\qquad\n \\sup_{\\substack{\\Theta\\in\\mathbb R\\\\|\\zeta|\\leq L^{C_4}}}\n       \\|\\mathcal T_\\zeta(\\Theta)\\|_{2\\to2}\\leq L^{-E_{\\rm id}}.$$ The family is independent of $\\Theta,\\zeta$; its defining parameters are independent of $J$.*\n\n\n\n*Split the big groups into two blocks. Every comparison frees nonempty sets of probes $A,B$ in the respective blocks, with coefficient $$\\label{cor:comparison-kernel}\n -(-1)^{|A|+|B|}\\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B).$$ Here $D_A,D_B$ are the free products, $X_A$ is the corresponding target-mark product, and $X_B$ the corresponding source-mark product. No shared or final $\\ell$ label enters these kernels. For a nonempty slot set $\\Lambda$ with at most $m_0$ slots in each group, $$\\label{cor:cell-kernel}\n \\mathcal K_\\Lambda(D,X)\n =\\sum_{j:\\nu_{\\Lambda j}\\geq\\xi}\n   \\frac{\\mathbf{1}_{\\log D\\in I_j}\\mathbf{1}_{\\log X\\in I_j}}{\\nu_{\\Lambda j}}\n   \\sum_{\\substack{\\chi\\text{ primitive}\\\\\\mathop{\\mathrm{cond}}(\\chi)\\leq Q}}\n        \\overline{\\chi(D)}\\chi(X),\n \\qquad I_j=[jh,(j+1)h).$$ The number $\\nu_{\\Lambda j}$ is the cell probability for the independent slot laws. The parameters $Q,h^{-1},\\xi^{-1}$ are fixed powers of $L$, with $h\\leq1$. There are at most $Q^2$ characters and $O_{m_0}(1+L^{d_0}\\log L/h)$ nonempty cells, and $$\\sup|\\mathcal K_\\Lambda|\\leq Q^2\\xi^{-1},\\qquad\n \\sup_X\\mathbb E_D|\\mathcal K_\\Lambda(D,X)|\\leq Q^2.$$ More precisely, for any prescribed fixed $A_0>0$, the parameters may be chosen, independently of $J$, so that for arbitrary $L^2$ tuple tests $f(X_B),g(X_A)$ and all $\\theta\\in\\mathbb R$, $|\\zeta|\\leq L^{C_4}$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\big|\\mathbb E\\,\\overline{f(X_B)}g(X_A)\n \\big[&(X_AX_B)^{i\\zeta}\\mathrm{e}(\\theta X_AX_B)\\notag\\\\\n &-\\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B)\n       (D_AD_B)^{i\\zeta}\\mathrm{e}(\\theta D_AD_B)\\big]\\big|\n \\leq L^{-A_0}\\|f\\|_2\\|g\\|_2.\n \\label{cor:comparison-contract}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The four label tuples in this expectation are independent; the tests need not depend only on their products. Expanding the two kernels, including all patterns, has total coefficient mass and number of terms bounded by fixed powers of $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-mellin",
        "exact_quote": "Let $a_w$ be supported on $w\\asymp Y$, where $Y=x^{1+o(1)}$, and suppose $H=x^{o(1)}\\to\\infty$. For every fixed integer $j\\geq1$, $$\\label{cor:local-mellin}\n \\int_{|\\beta|\\leq H^{-1}}|\\widehat a(\\beta)|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\beta\n \\ll_j\\frac1Y\\int_{\\mathbb R}(1+H|t|/Y)^{-j}\n       \\left|\\sum_w a_ww^{it}\\right|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm dt\n       +\\left(\\frac HY\\right)^2\\sum_w|a_w|^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-divisor-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H_{\\rm marks}(n)=\\mathcal W(n)W_{\\mathbf t}^{\\mathbf b}(n)$, where $0\\leq t_g\\leq m'$ for fixed $m'$ and $|\\mathbf b|\\leq1$. In particular, zero values of $t_g$ are allowed; these occur when a small mark is removed below. There is a divisor sum $$\\label{cor:divisor-model}\n H_0(n)=\\sum_{d'\\mid n}A_{d'},\\qquad\n d'\\leq\\exp(O(L^{.47})),\\qquad\n \\sum_{d'}\\frac{|A_{d'}|}{d'}\\leq L^{C_8},$$ whose prime divisors all belong to the active or inert groups, such that, on every positive integer dyad $n\\asymp R$, $$\\label{cor:model-error}\n \\sum_{n\\asymp R}|H_{\\rm marks}(n)-H_0(n)|^2\n       \\ll_N R L^{-N}$$ for every fixed $N$. The assertion is uniform in the bounded tuple test $\\mathbf b$, with no regularity assumption on that test.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **V_j(p)** ([source](#sec:intro)): Define $$V_j(p)=\frac{\log q_j(p)}{\log(p-1)}.$$
- **PD(1)** ([source](#eq:stick-breaking)): Their decreasing rearrangement $(L_1,L_2,\ldots)$ has the Poisson–Dirichlet distribution with parameter one, denoted $\mathop{\mathrm{PD}}(1)$.
- **active run** ([source](#mem:run-charge)): An *active run* is a maximal interval of consecutive active visits.
- **clean and dirty edges** ([source](#pm-h-21)): Call an edge *clean* if it stores and promotes no particle, and *dirty* otherwise.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r-prime-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-prime-estimates) / [proof](#stmt-lem-prime-estimates): Quantitative prime number theorem, Mertens and Siegel–Walfisz -> prime sums and progression counts with log-power errors
- **r-moment-bounds** [claim](#stmt-lem-moment-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-moment-bounds): Euler product for divisor sums; expansion of the square and Sobolev inequality for spaced points -> divisor moment and Dirichlet mean square bounds
- **r-rough-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-rough-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rough-counts): Bonferroni truncation of the divisibility events -> rough integer counts in intervals, progressions, characters
- **r-block-sieve** [claim](#stmt-lem-block-sieve) / [proof](#stmt-lem-block-sieve): Imported block sieve from the companion paper -> sieve with remainder over squarefree moduli
- **r-good-state** [claim](#stmt-det-good-state) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-good-state): Union bound and Markov inequality for the two good-state tests -> small bad set of root ratios
- **r-root-residue** [claim](#stmt-lem-root-residue) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-root-residue): Fourth moment bound for Kloosterman sums, discrepancy via Fourier smoothing, Euler factor averaging -> equidistribution of primitive roots with residues
- **r-root-replacement** [claim](#stmt-det-root-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-root-replacement): Bonferroni sandwich over unused primes and grid counting of boxes -> independent line model for path expansions
- **r-minor-moment** [claim](#stmt-prop-minor-moment) / [proof](#stmt-prop-minor-moment): Exact primewise memory identity, clean signed norm, dirty Schur bounds, rank expansion of distinctness -> trace moment bound and major-arc replacement
- **r-long-coefficient** [claim](#stmt-maj-long-coefficient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-maj-long-coefficient): Bonferroni approximation of rough numbers with prime and logarithmic phase estimates -> uniform smallness of the centered coefficient polynomial
- **r-sparse-frequency** [claim](#stmt-maj-sparse-frequency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-maj-sparse-frequency): Powering the small-prime polynomial and Gram matrix estimate -> sparse exceptional intervals with bounded energy
- **r-major-term** [claim](#stmt-prop-major-term) / [proof](#proof-stmt-maj-sparse-frequency-2): Character expansion, Mellin separation and splitting at small-prime polynomial size -> major sum saving
- **r-type-ii** [claim](#stmt-thm-type-ii) / [proof](#proof-stmt-maj-sparse-frequency-3): Cauchy inequality reduction, then minor and major arc estimates -> one-sided marked Type II estimate
- **r-graph-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-graph-transfer) / [proof](#stmt-prop-graph-transfer): Imported local transference and Schur bounds -> physical pairing bound from ideal norm
- **r-ideal-family** [claim](#stmt-prop-ideal-family) / [proof](#stmt-prop-ideal-family): Imported cell and character kernel family -> ideal norm bound for raw and comparison patterns
- **r-local-mellin** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-mellin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-mellin): Plancherel with a smooth bump and Fourier inversion in logarithmic coordinates -> local Mellin energy inequality
- **r-divisor-model** [claim](#stmt-lem-divisor-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-divisor-model): Truncated expansion of damping products with omega moment bound -> mean square approximation by a divisor sum
- **r-two-sided** [claim](#stmt-thm-two-sided) / [proof](#stmt-thm-two-sided): Graph transference, removal of shared labels, comparison multipliers, low and high Mellin frequencies -> two-sided correlation saving
- **r-progressions** [claim](#stmt-lem-progressions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-progressions): Represented unions, Bonferroni truncation and counting in one progression -> summed progression remainder
- **r-interior** [claim](#stmt-thm-interior) / [proof](#stmt-thm-interior): Presieve, replace prime slots by rough slots, allocation to two-sided correlation, removal of marks -> interior prime statistic asymptotics
- **r-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-progressions-2): Factorial measures, size-biased sampling, sorting and dyadic decomposition -> Poisson–Dirichlet limit for ordinary prime averages
- **r-pre-primes** [claim](#stmt-lp-pre-primes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-pre-primes): Prime number theorem lower bound -> enough primes in dyadic intervals
- **r-pre-derivatives** [claim](#stmt-lp-pre-derivatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-pre-derivatives): Summation by parts -> first-derivative exponential sum bound
- **r-mean-value** [claim](#stmt-lp-mean-value) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-mean-value): Congruence iteration in Linnik form with degeneracy and Vandermonde arguments -> degree-uniform power-sum bound
- **r-log-phase** [claim](#stmt-lp-log-phase) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-log-phase): Shifted averaging, Taylor expansion, box occupancy and Hölder with the mean value bound -> logarithmic phase cancellation on progressions
- **r-l-upper** [claim](#stmt-lp-l-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-l-upper): Partial summation and phase cancellation on dyads -> bound near the line of absolute convergence
- **r-local-log-derivative** [claim](#stmt-lp-local-log-derivative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-local-log-derivative): Jensen formula, Blaschke quotient and Harnack inequality -> local formula for the logarithmic derivative
- **r-zero-free** [claim](#stmt-lp-zero-free) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-zero-free): Positive trigonometric polynomial argument with the local formula -> zero-free strip
- **r-prime-polynomial** [claim](#stmt-lp-prime-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-zero-free-2): Smoothing, Mellin inversion and contour shift into the zero-free strip -> long prime polynomial cancellation

          "source_id": "PM-011.2"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-law-equal-weighting**: For every fixed k and every bounded continuous test function, the average over primes with equal weights of the test function applied to the k largest normalized logarithmic factors of p-1 tends to its expectation under the Poisson–Dirichlet law with parameter one. The limit is taken through all real x.
- **inv-ineffective-prime-inputs**: The prime-number estimates feeding the argument carry constants that are not claimed to be effective, so no explicit rate of convergence follows from the result.
- **inv-interior-statistic-limit**: The centered long-prime divisor statistic over shifted primes is negligible against the prime count for every admissible smooth cutoff, through all real x.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-key-crypto-bounds**: Use the theorem to certify concrete tail probabilities for the factor structure of p-1 at a fixed cryptographic key size. -> The result is an asymptotic limit law whose underlying prime estimates have non-effective constants, so no explicit error bound at a given size is available.
- **anti-recursive-pratt-levels**: Apply the same law again to the prime factors of p-1 and then to their predecessors, assuming independence across generations. -> The theorem concerns a prime chosen uniformly among all primes; a prime factor of p-1 is conditioned and not uniformly distributed, and later-generation independence is only a hypothesis.
- **anti-thin-prime-ensembles**: Transfer the limit law to a thin subfamily of primes such as primes of a special algebraic form. -> The statement is for ordinary equal weighting of all primes up to x; a restricted family can have a deterministic factor structure that contradicts the continuous limit.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is a non-constructive asymptotic limit theorem; its prime estimates are stated with constants that need not be effective, and no finite procedure with explicit steps is given, so no reference implementation with an independent oracle can faithfully be tied to it.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an asymptotic distributional limit with ineffective constants. It supplies no explicit procedure that a program could execute. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Poisson–Dirichlet stick-breaking description could drive a sampler for reference. The paper gives no simulation method, and the law is only a limit as x tends to infinity, so any such use is inspirational at best. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no reusable instrument or checker. Its estimates are internal analytic steps of one proof. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sieve and the operator bounds are proof devices with remainder terms. They are not algorithms with stated inputs, outputs and running times. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure appears. The memory particles and states are analytic bookkeeping for an operator identity. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is purely asymptotic with no computational kernel or cost model that hardware could accelerate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The factor-size law bears on smoothness heuristics for p-1, but the limit has no effective rate and the constants may be ineffective. It therefore cannot certify parameter choices at a fixed key size. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, model or dataset is involved. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper states no optimisation problem. Its parameters are chosen only to make error terms small. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no communication, concurrency or consensus content. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The contribution is a theorem of probabilistic number theory with no direct engineering construction. It supports heuristic runtime models for factoring only in the asymptotic regime. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-ineffective-constants** (approximation): The prime estimates carry constants that need not be effective, so the limit law comes with no explicit rate of convergence and no explicit threshold in x. Blocked use: ['certifying a concrete tail probability for the factor structure of p-1 at a fixed finite size', 'deriving explicit parameters for fixed key sizes from the theorem']
- **L-equal-weighting-sampling** (hypothesis): The main theorem is stated for ordinary equal weighting of the primes, with x tending to infinity through all real values and a fixed number of coordinates. Blocked use: ['applying the law to a thin family of primes such as primes of special form', 'applying the law with a number of coordinates growing with x']
- **L-root-level-only** (nonconstructive): The theorem supplies the factorization law only at the root of the Ford–Konyagin–Luca model; the recursive independence assumptions for later generations are additional hypotheses. Blocked use: ['iterating the Poisson–Dirichlet law on prime factors of p-1 and then on their predecessors', 'using the law for the factor structure of conditioned, non-uniform primes']
- **L-polynomial-size-factors** (formal_scope): The law concerns the logarithmic mass carried by prime factors of polynomial size; the small prime divisors are outside its scope. Blocked use: ['using the theorem for valuations of small prime divisors of p-1', 'using it for the number of prime factors below a subpower threshold']
- **L-type-ii-fixed-parameters** (hypothesis): The one-sided marked estimate needs fixed parameters and a number of marking bands large in terms of them; the implied constant and the threshold for x depend on all fixed parameters. Blocked use: ['applying the Type II bound with parameters that grow with x', 'reading the bound as uniform in the band exponents']
- **L-no-algorithm** (nonconstructive): The results are asymptotic estimates and a probabilistic limit theorem; no finite procedure with explicit steps or cost is stated. Blocked use: ['treating the sieve or operator bounds as an algorithm with a running time', 'using the theorem as a specification for a factoring or prime-generation routine']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For a prime <i>p</i> chosen uniformly from $`3\le p\le x`$, list the prime factors of $`p-1`$ in decreasing order, with multiplicity. As $`x\to\infty`$, their logarithms, divided by $`\log(p-1)`$, converge in every finite joint distribution to the Poisson–Dirichlet distribution with parameter one. This proves the conjecture of Ford, Konyagin and Luca.

<a id="sec:intro"></a>
### Introduction

The prime factors of a typical integer, viewed on a logarithmic scale, form a random partition of unit mass. We prove that the same limiting partition occurs for the predecessor of a uniformly chosen prime.

For a prime $p\ge3$, list the prime factors of $p-1$, with multiplicity, in decreasing order, $$q_1(p)\ge q_2(p)\ge\cdots,$$ and put $q_j(p)=1$ after the list is exhausted. Define $$V_j(p)=\frac{\log q_j(p)}{\log(p-1)}.$$ Then $V_j(p)\ge0$ and $\sum_jV_j(p)=1$.

<a id="eq:stick-breaking"></a>
Let $U_1,U_2,\ldots$ be independent uniform random variables on $(0,1)$, and form the stick fragments $$\label{eq:stick-breaking}
 B_1=1-U_1,
 \qquad B_j=\left(\prod_{i<j}U_i\right)(1-U_j)
       \quad(j\ge2).$$ Their decreasing rearrangement $(L_1,L_2,\ldots)$ has the Poisson–Dirichlet distribution with parameter one, denoted $\mathop{\mathrm{PD}}(1)$. Write $\pi(x)$ for the number of primes at most $x$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main-law"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *For every fixed $k\ge1$ and every bounded continuous function $F:[0,1]^k\to\mathbb R$, $$\label{eq:main-law}
 \lim_{x\to\infty}\frac1{\pi(x)-1}
     \sum_{\substack{3\le p\le x\\p\text{ prime}}}
       F\bigl(V_1(p),\ldots,V_k(p)\bigr)
 =\mathbb EF(L_1,\ldots,L_k).$$ The limit holds through all real $x$ and uses ordinary equal weighting of the primes.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves positively the conjecture of Ford, Konyagin and Luca (Ford, Konyagin, and Luca 2010, sec. 6, Conjecture 5) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[FordKonyaginLuca2010](#ref-FordKonyaginLuca2010)]. Their formulation uses the same multiplicity convention, normalization, and ordinary counting distribution on primes. The assertion includes all finite joint distributions, strengthening the largest-factor prediction alone.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### History and significance.

For ordinary uniformly sampled integers, the largest-factor law begins with Dickman (Dickman 1930) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Dickman](#ref-Dickman)] and the smooth-number estimates of de Bruijn (Bruijn 1951) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[deBruijn1951](#ref-deBruijn1951)]. Billingsley proved the joint limiting law of the ordered large prime divisors (Billingsley 1972) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Billingsley](#ref-Billingsley)]. Donnelly and Grimmett (Donnelly and Grimmett 1993, Theorem 1 and Corollaries 2–3) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[DonnellyGrimmett](#ref-DonnellyGrimmett)] gave a size-biased treatment, including multiplicities, leading directly to the stick-breaking and Poisson–Dirichlet descriptions. Arratia, Kochman and Miller (Arratia, Kochman, and Miller 2014) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ArratiaKochmanMiller2014](#ref-ArratiaKochmanMiller2014)] express this identification through the joint intensities of distinct factor tuples. Our final passage from interior factor statistics to ranked factors uses the same size-biased approach; that passage is proved explicitly in Section <a href="#pd:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pd:section">8</a>.

The shifted-prime problem has a different arithmetic difficulty: requiring a product of large primes to divide $p-1$ places $p$ in a progression with that product as modulus. Standard distribution estimates give useful information for products below a fixed power of $x$, whereas the full factor partition involves products throughout the range below $x$. The smoothness question arose in Erdős’s work on Euler’s function (Erdős 1935, 1956) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Erdos1935](#ref-Erdos1935), [Erdos1956](#ref-Erdos1956)], and Pomerance (Pomerance 1980) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Pomerance1980](#ref-Pomerance1980)] developed its connection with large totient fibers. Baker and Harman (Baker and Harman 1998, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BakerHarman1998](#ref-BakerHarman1998)] and Lichtman (Lichtman 2022, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Lichtman2022](#ref-Lichtman2022)] obtained lower bounds of the form $x/(\log x)^C$ for primes with smooth predecessors, at thresholds $x^{0.2961}$ for $p\le x$ and $x^{0.2844}$ for $x<p\le2x$, respectively.

<a id="eq:shifted-dickman"></a>
For shifted primes, Granville states the conjectural asymptotic (Granville 2008, sec. 5.3, Equation (5.1)) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Granville](#ref-Granville)] $$\label{eq:shifted-dickman}
 \#\{p\le x:P^+(p-1)\le x^{1/u}\}
       \sim\pi(x)\rho(u)\qquad(u\ge1\text{ fixed}),$$ where $P^+(n)$ is the largest prime factor, with $P^+(1)=1$, and the Dickman function is determined by $\rho(u)=1$ for $0\le u\le1$ and $u\rho'(u)+\rho(u-1)=0$ for $u>1$. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves this fixed-$u$ conjecture positively and proves the complete joint limiting law. The use of $x$ rather than $p-1$ in the threshold in <a href="#eq:shifted-dickman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:shifted-dickman">[eq:shifted-dickman]</a> causes no change: restrict first to $\varepsilon x<p\le x$, where $\log(p-1)/\log x\to1$ uniformly, and then let $\varepsilon\downarrow0$. The largest part of $\mathop{\mathrm{PD}}(1)$ has the continuous Dickman distribution, by the ordinary-integer limit in (Donnelly and Grimmett 1993, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[DonnellyGrimmett](#ref-DonnellyGrimmett)] and the smooth-number asymptotic in (Granville 2008, sec. 1, Equation (1.1)) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Granville](#ref-Granville)]; hence the corresponding threshold sandwich applies.

The closest general distribution theorem is due to Bharadwaj and Rodgers (Bharadwaj and Rodgers 2026, Theorem 7) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[BharadwajRodgers2026](#ref-BharadwajRodgers2026)]. Their Poisson–Dirichlet theorem applies to nonnegative sequences satisfying their regularity and congruence-uniformity hypotheses, with level of distribution one. For shifted primes this gives the full law under the Elliott–Halberstam conjecture, as anticipated in (Ford, Konyagin, and Luca 2010, sec. 6) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[FordKonyaginLuca2010](#ref-FordKonyaginLuca2010)]. Unconditionally, (Bharadwaj and Rodgers 2026, Proposition 2 and Lemma 8) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[BharadwajRodgers2026](#ref-BharadwajRodgers2026)] give level one half and the corresponding factor correlations for tests supported where the sum of the logarithmic coordinates is less than one half. Our extraction reaches every compact subset of the open simplex with coordinate sum less than one, and the final probability argument controls its boundary. All smoothness parameters in <a href="#eq:shifted-dickman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:shifted-dickman">[eq:shifted-dickman]</a> remain fixed as $x$ grows.

There is also strong recent information about the small prime divisors of shifted primes. Ford (Ford 2025) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Ford2025](#ref-Ford2025)] proves a total-variation approximation for their valuations on subpower ranges; Gorodetsky (Gorodetsky 2026) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Gorodetsky2026](#ref-Gorodetsky2026)] obtains such approximations within a general sieve model. The large-factor law studied here concerns the logarithmic mass carried by prime factors of polynomial size.

Ford, Konyagin and Luca formulated their conjecture in studying prime chains and Pratt trees. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> supplies the factorization law at the root of their probabilistic model; the recursive independence assumptions used for later generations are additional hypotheses. Smooth predecessors also underlie the Carmichael-number construction of Alford, Granville and Pomerance (Alford, Granville, and Pomerance 1994) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[AlfordGranvillePomerance1994](#ref-AlfordGranvillePomerance1994)], together with a separate progression-distribution input.

The companion article *Weighted dilation graphs, smooth shifted primes and totient fibers* (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted)], denoted S, proves that, for every fixed positive smoothness exponent, there are $x^{1-o(1)}$ primes in its stated interval with smooth predecessors. Such a lower bound does not imply a positive limiting proportion, much less <a href="#eq:main-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main-law">[eq:main-law]</a>. We use S’s dilation-graph operator theorems in a different extraction argument. Section <a href="#sec:correlations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:correlations">6</a> states their precise contracts and verifies the new endpoint hypotheses. The long-prime polynomial and logarithmic-phase estimates needed in both analytic branches are proved in Appendix <a href="#lp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lp:section">9</a>; Section <a href="#sec:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:inputs">2</a> records the ranges in which we use them.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The new analytic step.

The main new estimate is Theorem <a href="#thm:type-II" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:type-II">8</a>: a marked Type II estimate in which only the predecessor side carries divisor marks, namely weights recording selected small prime divisors. It permits a prime factor on the other side to be replaced by a rough integer, free of primes below a prescribed cutoff, with its local density. Cauchy’s inequality, applied after dividing out one tuple of marks, leads to a small-determinant relation between primitive lattice vectors. We average its long signed moments using a root-residue formula and an exact memory expansion. The memory records a prime across gaps between its uses, so its divisibility probability is charged only once.

The root coordinates are restricted by quantitative separation conditions. These give both a balanced lattice box for fresh-label minor arcs and a separation rule for retrieving remembered labels. Global distinctness is then restored by grouping equalities between newly drawn prime labels according to their rank, the number of independent equality constraints. High ranks supply many small point-probability factors, whereas low ranks alter only a vanishing fraction of the signed edge contractions. The resulting estimate has arbitrarily strong fixed logarithmic precision, with a number of marking bands independent of their particular fixed exponents.

The signed-moment method has precedents in divisibility graphs. Helfgott and Radziwiłł center prime-divisibility weights by subtracting $1/p$ and analyze high traces through prime-labelled walks (Helfgott and Radziwiłł 2021, secs. 1.3, 1.5 and 5) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[HelfgottRadziwill2021](#ref-HelfgottRadziwill2021)]. Their proposed composite shifts (Helfgott and Radziwiłł 2021, sec. 9.1) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[HelfgottRadziwill2021](#ref-HelfgottRadziwill2021)] are developed by Pilatte using products of primes and a non-backtracking trace estimate (Pilatte 2026, Definition 3.2 and Proposition 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Pilatte2026](#ref-Pilatte2026)]. Recurring prime labels create dependencies in these walk expansions. Here the state contains active prime lists and a primitive lattice vector. At each prime $p$, root averaging gives a uniform projective line, so a specified line has probability $1/(p+1)$. The memory preserves that line across gaps between active runs. Sections <a href="#sec:determinant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:determinant">[sec:determinant]</a>–<a href="#mem:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mem:section">4</a> develop this determinant geometry and prove the exact memory identity; Section <a href="#sec:major-arcs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:major-arcs">5</a> supplies the complementary major-term estimate.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### From marked correlations to the full law.

The second estimate, Theorem <a href="#thm:two-sided" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:two-sided">16</a>, uses marks on both endpoints. We apply the general graph inputs of S to a centered long-prime divisor statistic and prove the required new endpoint Fourier estimates. A small mark restricts difficult frequencies to a sparse set; a long-prime polynomial controls the positive tuple term there, and a finite divisor model controls the constant term.

A first-moment presieve and the one-sided estimate replace all prime slots of the composite terms by rough slots. Successful marking groups then bring these composite terms within the two-sided estimate. Averaging over disjoint fixed marking arrays removes the marks from the prime average. This proves interior factorial-moment asymptotics for ordinary prime dyads.

Finally, sequential size-biased sampling turns the interior factorial measures into probability densities of total mass one. This excludes escape to the boundary and yields the stick fragments in <a href="#eq:stick-breaking" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stick-breaking">[eq:stick-breaking]</a>. The expected undrawn mass controls sorting, and a finite dyadic decomposition gives all real upper endpoints. We provide this probability argument explicitly, since restricted-support moment formulas alone are not the statement of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-map">1</a> summarizes the dependence of these steps. The two-sided endpoint analysis is in Section <a href="#sec:correlations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:correlations">6</a>, the prime extraction and removal of marks in Section <a href="#ext:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:section">7</a>, and the probability argument in Section <a href="#pd:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pd:section">8</a>.

<figure id="fig:proof-map">

<figcaption>The proof has two analytic inputs. The one-sided estimate replaces prime slots in composite terms by rough slots. Successful marking brings the resulting centered statistic under the two-sided estimate. Averaging the fixed marks then gives interior moments; the probability argument recovers the full law. Appendix <a href="#lp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lp:section">9</a> supplies the long-prime and logarithmic-phase estimates used in the analytic branches.</figcaption>
</figure>
